````text
```

For the r4 run, I need to modify a few key parameters: switching the reward device from cpu to cuda for better performance, increasing the checkpoint limit to 40, and setting up checkpoint resumption from the previous run's output directory.

I'm debating whether to use the backup checkpoint path or the original one—the original checkpoint-100 should still be accessible in the output directory, so I'll go with that for simplicity. The resume_from_checkpoint parameter can point to any checkpoint directory regardless of the output_dir, which is where new checkpoints will be saved going forward. I'll use the backup checkpoint path to be safe, and set up a new output directory for r4 at `/root/autodl-tmp/grpo_flatnli_r4`. Since the task specifies gpu_ids with cuda:3, I need to set CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3, and I'll construct the training command using ssh_execute with nohup since submit_training_job isn't available.

Constructing the command... I need to wrap this command properly with conda activation and nohup so it runs in the background correctly.
````

```text
The trainer calls `trainer.train()` at line 510 without resume_from_checkpoint. HF Trainer supports `trainer.train(resume_from_checkpoint=path)`, but the custom script doesn't expose that as a CLI arg.

I have two options:
1. Add `--resume_from_checkpoint` as an argparse argument and pass it to `trainer.train()`
2. Use HF Trainer's auto-resume feature: if `output_dir` contains checkpoints, HF Trainer can automatically resume from the latest one

For option 2, I can copy the checkpoint-100 into the new output_dir, or point output_dir to the old one. 

The cleanest approach is to add the CLI argument. Now I need to check the argparse section around lines 330-380 and the train call at line 510 to implement this properly.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
OK, so PID 67045 is actually the bash wrapper, not the python process itself. Looking at the log more carefully:

There are two phases of log output visible:
1. First phase (from PID 66504's failed run) - the HF connection failures, but then...
2. Wait, actually the log was overwritten by the new run (using `>` redirect). Let me read more carefully.

Looking at the timestamps:
- `23:29:11` - still retrying for tokenizer_config.json
- `23:29:21` - now retrying for config.json (tokenizer loaded from cache after retries)


- `23:29:27` - NLI model starts loading
- `23:29:31` - NLI model successfully loaded with entailment index 1, and stage 2 training begins with the flat_nli reward and tau schedule
- `23:29:33` - GRPO training kicks off and the progress bar shows it's actually running

But there's something odd here - I'm seeing retry failures for config.json continuing through `23:29:32`, which overlaps with when training supposedly started.

Looking more carefully at the logs, I think what happened is the second run appended to the same log file, and the null bytes scattered throughout are leftover tqdm output from the first failed attempt. The actual successful run is the one with those clean log entries at `23:29:27` through `23:29:33` - that's when the NLI model loaded, stage 2 initialized, and training actually began.

But there's something odd here: the progress bar shows `0/756 [00:00<?, ?it/s]`, which means it's starting from step 0, not resuming from step 100 like it should be. The `resume_from_checkpoint` parameter was supposed to load from `/root/autodl-tmp/grpo_flatnli_r3/checkpoint-100`, but since the new output directory is empty, I'm wondering if the checkpoint loading actually worked or if it just started fresh.

Actually, HF Trainer typically shows `0/756` initially and then jumps to the correct step once it loads the optimizer state, so I should wait a bit longer to see if that happens. The retry failures in the logs are probably just from the initial HEAD request before falling back to cache—the NLI model loaded fine from cache at 23:29:31, so that's not a real blocker.

The null bytes and out-of-order timestamps in the log are likely from tqdm progress bar output getting mixed in, which happens when using `>` redirect. I should wait a couple of minutes and check the logs again to see the actual training progress and step timing.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The key data point is: `13%|█▎ | 101/756 [00:56<06:09, 1.77it/s]`

This tells us:
- Training resumed from step 100 (showing 101/756 which is the first step after resume)
- **101 steps in 56 seconds** — wait no, it says `101/756 [00:56<06:09, 1.77it/s]`, which means it's at step 101 out of 756, elapsed time 56s. But since it resumed from checkpoint-100, the first step (100→101) took about 56s.


- The progress bar started when training resumed, so the 56 seconds includes checkpoint loading time. The speed of 1.77 it/s seems suspiciously fast for GRPO training with generation and NLI reward computation — that would be less than 0.6 seconds per step, which doesn't quite add up.

- I should account for the fact that each tqdm step might represent an optimizer step (16 accumulation steps), not individual generation steps, which would make the timing more reasonable. The first step also likely includes compilation warmup overhead.

- I need to wait for more data to get a stable estimate of actual training speed rather than relying on this initial measurement.
```
